By providing a challenging benchmark and standardized evaluation protocols, the authors aim to accelerate progress in this area. The finding that current LLMs struggle with semantic reasoning and explanation, especially with longer sequences, is crucial. It underscores the need for more specialized architectures and training techniques that can effectively handle spatiotemporal data and infer higher-level semantic meanings.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-Defined Problem:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of semantic understanding in mobility data and its limitations in current research.
    *   **Comprehensive Dataset:** The dataset is well-constructed, diverse in question types and trajectory granularities, and carefully annotated. The inclusion of free-form questions is particularly valuable for assessing higher-level reasoning.
    *   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The evaluation protocols are well-defined and include appropriate metrics for each question type, addressing the subjectivity of free-form answers using the LLM-as-a-judge framework.
    *   **Thorough Analysis:** The experimental results provide valuable insights into the strengths and weaknesses of various LLMs on mobility question answering tasks. The analysis of how trajectory length and semantic information affect performance is insightful.
    *   **Reproducibility:** The authors have taken steps to ensure reproducibility, which enhances the credibility and usefulness of the dataset and benchmark.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Dataset Scope:** The dataset is based on Geolife data, which is geographically limited to Beijing. This limits its generalizability to other regions with different mobility patterns and cultural contexts.
    *   **Dependency on LLMs:** The use of LLMs for evaluation, while appropriate, also introduces dependencies on the capabilities and potential biases of those models. This is partly mitigated by using various LLMs and carefully considering their limitations.
    *   **Textual Representation:** The conversion of GPS data to text might lose some spatiotemporal information, and its effectiveness is also very dependent on the tokenizer of the LLM.
*   **Potential Influence:** MobQA has the potential to be a highly influential benchmark dataset for the mobility data analysis and spatiotemporal reasoning communities. It can drive research toward developing more sophisticated LLMs that can truly "understand" human movement, leading to significant advancements in various applications. However, the community needs to consider carefully the limitations of the dataset scope (Geolife), and potential LLM biases, to avoid these limitations being transferred to downstream applications.

Score: 8

**Rationale:** The paper presents a significant contribution by introducing a novel benchmark dataset and evaluation framework for semantic understanding of human mobility data. While there are limitations in terms of dataset scope and reliance on LLMs, the paper is well-written, the problem is clearly defined, and the analysis is thorough. The potential influence of MobQA on advancing research in this area is substantial, justifying a score of 8. It doesn’t achieve a higher score because of the aforementioned scope limitations and reliance on a single dataset with data from one geographical area.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Semi-supervised Image Dehazing via Expectation-Maximization and Bidirectional Brownian Bridge Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11165v1)
- **Authors**: Bing Liu, Le Wang, Mingming Liu, Hao Liu, Rui Yao, Yong Zhou, Peng Liu, Tongqiang Xia
- **Abstract**: Existing dehazing methods deal with real-world haze images with difficulty, especially scenes with thick haze. One of the main reasons is the lack of real-world paired data and robust priors. To avoid the costly collection of paired hazy and clear images, we propose an efficient semi-supervised image dehazing method via Expectation-Maximization and Bidirectional Brownian Bridge Diffusion Models (EM-B3DM) with a two-stage learning scheme. In the first stage, we employ the EM algorithm to decouple the joint distribution of paired hazy and clear images into two conditional distributions, which are then modeled using a unified Brownian Bridge diffusion model to directly capture the structural and content-related correlations between hazy and clear images. In the second stage, we leverage the pre-trained model and large-scale unpaired hazy and clear images to further improve the performance of image dehazing. Additionally, we introduce a detail-enhanced Residual Difference Convolution block (RDC) to capture gradient-level information, significantly enhancing the model's representation capability. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our EM-B3DM achieves superior or at least comparable performance to state-of-the-art methods on both synthetic and real-world datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary**

The paper introduces a novel semi-supervised image dehazing method called EM-B3DM (Expectation-Maximization and Bidirectional Brownian Bridge Diffusion Models). The core idea is to address the challenges of real-world image dehazing, which often suffers from a lack of paired hazy and clear images and robust priors.  The method utilizes a two-stage learning scheme. In the first stage, an EM algorithm decouples the joint distribution of hazy and clear images into two conditional distributions modeled by a unified Brownian Bridge diffusion model. This aims to capture structural and content correlations between hazy and clear images. The second stage leverages the pre-trained model and large-scale unpaired hazy and clear images to further improve dehazing performance. The paper also introduces a detail-enhanced Residual Difference Convolution (RDC) block to capture gradient-level information and boost the model's representational capacity. Experiments on synthetic and real-world datasets demonstrate that EM-B3DM achieves superior or comparable performance to state-of-the-art methods.

**Critical Evaluation**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of Expectation-Maximization with Brownian Bridge diffusion models for semi-supervised dehazing is a relatively novel approach.  Using Brownian Bridges to enforce structure consistency during the diffusion process is a good idea. The RDC block, while not entirely groundbreaking, provides a targeted enhancement for detail preservation, a crucial aspect in dehazing. The use of a two-stage training approach is also a helpful strategy given the nature of the problem. The rigorous derivation for decoupling the joint distribution based on the EM algorithm to avoid relying on large amounts of paired data is an additional strength.

*   **Significance:** Image dehazing is a relevant and well-studied problem in computer vision.  The paper's contribution is significant because it addresses a key limitation of existing deep learning methods: their reliance on large amounts of paired training data, which are difficult to obtain in real-world scenarios. The semi-supervised approach allows the model to leverage readily available unpaired data, increasing its practicality and generalization capabilities. Improved performance on real-world datasets suggests that the method is effective in addressing the domain gap between synthetic and real hazy images.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Theoretical Foundation:** The paper is well-grounded in theory, providing a clear justification for using the EM algorithm and Brownian Bridge diffusion models.
    *   **Practical Application:** The semi-supervised approach makes the method more practical for real-world deployment, where paired data is scarce.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experiments cover both synthetic and real-world datasets, and the method is compared against a range of state-of-the-art techniques. The ablation studies are helpful in understanding the contribution of different components of the model.
    *   **Clear Writing:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** Diffusion models are computationally intensive, and the EM-B3DM method is likely to be more complex and slower than simpler dehazing algorithms. Although the authors mention efficiency and using 200 sampling steps, a direct comparison of computational cost with other methods would be beneficial.
    *   **RDC Novelty:** The RDC block, while effective, does not appear to be a substantial departure from existing detail enhancement techniques in CNNs. The innovation may come from integrating it specifically in the dehazing context. More detailed justification or architectural nuance may have improved this contribution.
    *   **Limited Exploration of Unpaired Data:** The paper mentions the importance of large-scale unpaired data but doesn't fully explore different strategies for leveraging this data (e.g., comparing different loss functions or training schedules for the unpaired data).
    *   **Dependence on pre-trained VQGAN:** The performance of EM-B3DM relies heavily on a pre-trained VQGAN model. While this is not necessarily a flaw, it is important to acknowledge this dependence and discuss its potential limitations.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of image dehazing by demonstrating the effectiveness of combining EM with diffusion models for semi-supervised learning. The work could spur further research into developing more efficient and robust dehazing algorithms that can leverage readily available unpaired data. The design of the detail-enhanced RDC could also inspire other researchers to develop more effective feature extraction techniques for dehazing.

**Justification for Score:**

While the paper presents a novel and theoretically sound approach to semi-supervised image dehazing, its impact is somewhat limited by its complexity and reliance on a pre-trained VQGAN. The RDC block is a helpful addition, but does not represent a significant breakthrough. The experimental results are promising, but a more thorough analysis of computational cost and a deeper exploration of unpaired data strategies would strengthen the paper. Taking all these aspects into consideration, this paper is of good contribution, therefore it merits a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Personalized Distractor Generation via MCTS-Guided Reasoning Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11184v1)
- **Authors**: Tao Wu, Jingyuan Chen, Wang Lin, Jian Zhan, Mengze Li, Kun Kuang, Fei Wu
- **Abstract**: Distractors, incorrect but plausible answer choices in multiple-choice questions (MCQs), play a critical role in educational assessment by diagnosing student misconceptions. Recent work has leveraged large language models (LLMs) to generate shared, group-level distractors by learning common error patterns across large student populations. However, such distractors often fail to capture the diverse reasoning errors of individual students, limiting their diagnostic effectiveness. To address this limitation, we introduce the task of personalized distractor generation, which aims to generate tailored distractors based on individual misconceptions inferred from each student's past question-answering (QA) records, ensuring every student receives options that effectively exposes their specific reasoning errors. While promising, this task is challenging because each student typically has only a few QA records, which often lack the student's underlying reasoning processes, making training-based group-level approaches infeasible. To overcome this, we propose a training-free two-stage framework. In the first stage, we construct a student-specific misconception prototype by applying Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to recover the student's reasoning trajectories from past incorrect answers. In the second stage, this prototype guides the simulation of the student's reasoning on new questions, enabling the generation of personalized distractors that align with the student's recurring misconceptions. Experiments show that our approach achieves the best performance in generating plausible, personalized distractors for 140 students, and also effectively generalizes to group-level settings, highlighting its robustness and adaptability.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of generating personalized distractors (incorrect but plausible answer options) for multiple-choice questions (MCQs) to better diagnose individual student misconceptions. Recognizing that existing methods primarily generate group-level distractors that fail to capture individual reasoning errors, the authors propose a training-free, two-stage framework. The first stage reconstructs student-specific misconceptions using Monte Carlo Tree Search (MCTS) to analyze past question-answering records. The second stage uses this reconstructed misconception profile to guide the generation of personalized distractors for new questions. The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of their approach in generating plausible, personalized distractors and its generalizability to group-level settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel and important task: Personalized Distractor Generation. While distractor generation itself is not new, the focus on tailoring distractors to *individual* student misconceptions is a significant departure from group-level approaches. The use of MCTS to reconstruct student reasoning trajectories from limited and potentially noisy data (selected answers only) is also a novel application of this technique. The proposed two-stage framework is well-structured and addresses the key challenges of the task.

**Significance:** The work has significant implications for educational assessment and personalized learning. By generating personalized distractors, the system can provide more accurate diagnoses of student misconceptions, leading to more effective interventions. The training-free nature of the approach is particularly valuable, as it avoids the need for large-scale labeled datasets, which are often unavailable in real-world educational settings. The framework's generalizability to group-level distractor generation further enhances its practical applicability. The empirical evaluation, while thorough, could be strengthened (see weaknesses below).

**Strengths:**

*   **Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly defines and motivates the importance of personalized distractor generation.
*   **Methodology:** The two-stage framework is well-designed and technically sound. The use of MCTS is particularly clever for reasoning reconstruction.
*   **Training-Free Approach:** Avoiding reliance on large datasets makes the method more practical and accessible.
*   **Generalizability:** Demonstrating the framework's performance in group-level settings expands its potential impact.
*   **Empirical Validation:** The experimental results, ablation studies, and user study support the effectiveness of the approach.
*   **Clear Exposition:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Dataset Diversity:** While the paper curates two datasets, their diversity is somewhat limited. More data from diverse backgrounds and topics would strengthen the validation.
*   **LLM Dependency:** The framework relies heavily on the reasoning and generation abilities of LLMs. While the training-free aspect is good, the quality of the distractors is inherently tied to the performance of the underlying LLM, and this constraint should be acknowledged more. The generalizability across different LLM architectures, however, is explored and is a strength.
*   **Evaluation Metrics:** The LLM-based evaluation metrics (Plausibility and Coherence) are subjective and can be influenced by the LLM's biases. User studies with educators or more direct measures of diagnostic accuracy could add further weight. The current user study had a relatively small sample size.
*   **Justification of MCTS Implementation:** Although MCTS is a good choice, the justification is shallow and missing comparisons with other techniques for the same task.
*   **Limited Comparison in Group-Level Generation:** The comparison for group-level generation is limited to the comparison with three baselines and only based on Recall and requires deeper investigation.
*   **Minor Omissions:** The paper is missing an analysis of the impact of a personalized distractor generation approach on different student ability segments.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of educational assessment by introducing a novel task and a practical, effective approach. The use of MCTS for reasoning reconstruction and the training-free nature of the framework are significant strengths. However, the reliance on LLMs, the limitations in dataset diversity and evaluation metrics, and the need for further comparative analysis hold the paper back from achieving a higher score.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generating Dialogues from Egocentric Instructional Videos for Task Assistance: Dataset, Method and Benchmark
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11192v1)
- **Authors**: Lavisha Aggarwal, Vikas Bahirwani, Lin Li, Andrea Colaco
- **Abstract**: Many everyday tasks ranging from fixing appliances, cooking recipes to car maintenance require expert knowledge, especially when tasks are complex and multi-step. Despite growing interest in AI agents, there is a scarcity of dialogue-video datasets grounded for real world task assistance. In this paper, we propose a simple yet effective approach that transforms single-person instructional videos into task-guidance two-person dialogues, aligned with fine grained steps and video-clips. Our fully automatic approach, powered by large language models, offers an efficient alternative to the substantial cost and effort required for human-assisted data collection. Using this technique, we build HowToDIV, a large-scale dataset containing 507 conversations, 6636 question-answer pairs and 24 hours of videoclips across diverse tasks in cooking, mechanics, and planting. Each session includes multi-turn conversation where an expert teaches a novice user how to perform a task step by step, while observing user's surrounding through a camera and microphone equipped wearable device. We establish the baseline benchmark performance on HowToDIV dataset through Gemma-3 model for future research on this new task of dialogues for procedural-task assistance.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces HowToDIV, a large-scale dataset designed for training AI agents that can assist users with procedural tasks through dialogue.  The core idea is to automatically transform single-person instructional videos into two-person dialogues. The method leverages large language models (LLMs) to generate realistic conversations between a novice user and an expert based on the video content, step annotations, and subtitles.  The resulting dataset includes a significant amount of video and dialogue data across cooking, mechanical tasks, and planting. The paper also establishes baseline performance on the HowToDIV dataset using the Gemma-3 model and provides metrics for future research. The dataset and code are publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the *automatic* generation of a large-scale task-assistance *dialogue* dataset from existing instructional videos, rather than relying on manual data collection.  While existing datasets have focused on instructional videos, they often lack the interactive, multi-turn dialogue structure necessary for real-time task assistance. The specific prompt engineering used to achieve this is, however, relatively straightforward application of existing LLM capabilities. The combination of leveraging existing monologue videos *and* automatic conversion to dialogues is a solid contribution. The construction of the dataset using readily available resources is a significant plus.
*   **Significance:** Task-assistance dialogue datasets are crucial for developing AI agents that can effectively guide users through complex procedures. HowToDIV addresses a significant gap in this area. The size and diversity of the dataset make it a valuable resource for training and evaluating language-vision models for procedural task assistance.  The dataset can potentially accelerate research on conversational AI agents and improve their ability to understand user needs, provide step-by-step instructions, and address errors. The establishment of a benchmark performance also provides a reference for future advancements.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Scale and Diversity:** The dataset is substantial in size and covers a reasonable range of tasks, making it more broadly applicable than smaller, task-specific datasets.
    *   **Automation:** The automated generation process is a major strength. It avoids the high cost and time consumption of manual annotation.
    *   **Realism:** By using LLMs to generate dialogues based on video content, the dataset achieves a degree of realism that is difficult to replicate through purely synthetic data generation. The incorporation of error cases (derived from the EgoPER dataset) further enhances realism.
    *   **Baselines Provided:** Establishing baselines with a recent LLM provides a starting point for future research.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on LLMs:** The quality of the generated dialogues is limited by the capabilities of the underlying LLM. While LLMs are powerful, they can still generate inaccurate or nonsensical responses. The paper acknowledges this limitation.
    *   **Limited Multi-Modality in Benchmarking:** The paper focuses on text-based benchmarking with the Gemma-3 model. A more thorough evaluation would include models that can directly process video input and reason about user actions.
    *   **Simplistic Video Localization:** The video localization approach is quite basic, relying on subtitle timestamps. More sophisticated techniques for aligning video segments with dialogue turns could improve the quality of the dataset.
    *   **Evaluation Metrics:** While standard metrics like BLEU and ROUGE are used, they may not fully capture the nuances of task-oriented dialogue. The inclusion of LLM-as-a-Judge is helpful but subjective. Metrics that directly assess task completion and user satisfaction would be more informative.
    *   **No actual interaction**. The dialogues are synthetic, created by feeding the instructions to an LLM. There is no user feedback or corrections being modeled in the dataset.

*   **Potential Influence:** HowToDIV has the potential to become a widely used benchmark in the field of task-oriented dialogue and language-vision models. It could stimulate research on more sophisticated methods for generating realistic task-assistance dialogues and for training AI agents that can effectively guide users through complex procedures.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper provides a useful resource to the field. The automated dataset generation is a welcome alternative to manual creation. However, the reliance on existing LLM technology for dialogue generation, and basic nature of video processing and the simple benchmarks, limit the impact somewhat.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### StyleMM: Stylized 3D Morphable Face Model via Text-Driven Aligned Image Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11203v1)
- **Authors**: Seungmi Lee, Kwan Yun, Junyong Noh
- **Abstract**: We introduce StyleMM, a novel framework that can construct a stylized 3D Morphable Model (3DMM) based on user-defined text descriptions specifying a target style. Building upon a pre-trained mesh deformation network and a texture generator for original 3DMM-based realistic human faces, our approach fine-tunes these models using stylized facial images generated via text-guided image-to-image (i2i) translation with a diffusion model, which serve as stylization targets for the rendered mesh. To prevent undesired changes in identity, facial alignment, or expressions during i2i translation, we introduce a stylization method that explicitly preserves the facial attributes of the source image. By maintaining these critical attributes during image stylization, the proposed approach ensures consistent 3D style transfer across the 3DMM parameter space through image-based training. Once trained, StyleMM enables feed-forward generation of stylized face meshes with explicit control over shape, expression, and texture parameters, producing meshes with consistent vertex connectivity and animatability. Quantitative and qualitative evaluations demonstrate that our approach outperforms state-of-the-art methods in terms of identity-level facial diversity and stylization capability. The code and videos are available at [kwanyun.github.io/stylemm_page](kwanyun.github.io/stylemm_page).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the StyleMM paper:

**Summary:**

The StyleMM paper introduces a novel framework for creating stylized 3D Morphable Models (3DMMs) using text-guided image translation. The core idea is to leverage diffusion models to generate stylized facial images from text prompts and then use these images as training targets for fine-tuning a pre-trained mesh deformation network and a texture generator. A key contribution is an "Explicit Attribute-preserving Stylization" (EAS) module that maintains facial attributes like identity, expression, and pose during image stylization, preventing unwanted changes during the translation process.  StyleMM ensures consistent vertex connectivity for animatability, disentangled shape and texture control, and the ability to generate faces with styles extending beyond realistic human appearances. A consistent displacement loss is also proposed to maintain identity level diversity and prevent mode collapse. The method shows promising qualitative and quantitative results, demonstrating improved diversity and stylization capabilities compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of text-to-image generation with 3DMM fine-tuning is a relatively novel approach.  While text-to-image methods for 3D face generation exist, directly incorporating them into training a *parametric* model with disentangled control is less common. The EAS module is a significant contribution to explicitly address the problem of attribute drift during stylization. Consistent Displacement Loss also addresses a limitation of previous methods.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial gap in 3D face modeling: the creation of stylized, animatable, and controllable face models without relying on extensive stylized 3D datasets or manual art creation. This has significant potential for applications in animation, game development, and virtual reality. By enabling text-driven stylization, the method democratizes the creation of customized avatars and characters. The detailed evaluation provides valuable insights into the strengths and limitations of the method, paving the way for future research.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Complete Pipeline:** StyleMM offers a complete and well-structured pipeline from text prompt to stylized 3DMM.
    *   **Disentangled Control:** The framework effectively maintains disentangled control over shape, expression, and texture.
    *   **Identity Preservation:** The EAS module significantly improves identity preservation during stylization, which is a key challenge in existing methods.
    *   **Good Results:** Qualitative results look visually appealing and show good stylization. Quantitatively, the model performs well in metrics like face diversity and style score.
    *   **Clear Explanation and Evaluation:** The paper is clearly written, with well-defined metrics and thorough ablation studies.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:**  The method relies heavily on the quality and expressiveness of pre-trained models (FLAME, StyleGAN2, SDXL). Limitations of these models can propagate to the stylized 3DMM.
    *   **Geometric Consistency Trade-off:** As the paper acknowledges, there's a trade-off between stylization capability and geometric consistency. Extreme stylization can lead to misalignment issues. Although EAS is a solution, further improvements are desired.
    *   **Self-Intersections:** The generated meshes are susceptible to self intersections under exaggerated expressions.
    *   **Qualitative limitations:** It is unknown if the qualitative evaluations are evaluated using blind evaluation methods

*   **Potential Impact:** StyleMM has the potential to influence the field of 3D face modeling by enabling a more accessible and controllable approach to stylized avatar creation.  The proposed EAS module could also be adopted in other applications involving image translation where attribute preservation is critical. The clear evaluation methodology provides a solid foundation for future research.

**Justification of Score:**

While StyleMM exhibits several strengths, including its innovative pipeline and effective style transfer capabilities, some of the weaknesses such as heavy reliance on pretrained models, geometric consistency tradeoff, and generation of self intersecting mesh limit its overall impact on the field. Further improvement in the robustness and generalization performance would likely improve the score.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Efficient Image-to-Image Schrödinger Bridge for CT Field of View Extension
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11211v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenhao Li, Long Yang, Xiaojie Yin, Haijun Yu, Jiazhou Wang, Hongbin Han, Weigang Hu, Yixing Huang
- **Abstract**: Computed tomography (CT) is a cornerstone imaging modality for non-invasive, high-resolution visualization of internal anatomical structures. However, when the scanned object exceeds the scanner's field of view (FOV), projection data are truncated, resulting in incomplete reconstructions and pronounced artifacts near FOV boundaries. Conventional reconstruction algorithms struggle to recover accurate anatomy from such data, limiting clinical reliability. Deep learning approaches have been explored for FOV extension, with diffusion generative models representing the latest advances in image synthesis. Yet, conventional diffusion models are computationally demanding and slow at inference due to their iterative sampling process. To address these limitations, we propose an efficient CT FOV extension framework based on the image-to-image Schr\"odinger Bridge (I$^2$SB) diffusion model. Unlike traditional diffusion models that synthesize images from pure Gaussian noise, I$^2$SB learns a direct stochastic mapping between paired limited-FOV and extended-FOV images. This direct correspondence yields a more interpretable and traceable generative process, enhancing anatomical consistency and structural fidelity in reconstructions. I$^2$SB achieves superior quantitative performance, with root-mean-square error (RMSE) values of 49.8\,HU on simulated noisy data and 152.0HU on real data, outperforming state-of-the-art diffusion models such as conditional denoising diffusion probabilistic models (cDDPM) and patch-based diffusion methods. Moreover, its one-step inference enables reconstruction in just 0.19s per 2D slice, representing over a 700-fold speedup compared to cDDPM (135s) and surpassing diffusionGAN (0.58s), the second fastest. This combination of accuracy and efficiency makes I$^2$SB highly suitable for real-time or clinical deployment.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an efficient deep learning framework, I2SB (Image-to-Image Schrodinger Bridge), for extending the field of view (FOV) in computed tomography (CT) images.  It addresses the problem of data truncation when the scanned object exceeds the scanner's FOV, leading to image artifacts and incomplete reconstructions. I2SB, based on Schrodinger Bridge diffusion models, learns a direct stochastic mapping between limited-FOV and extended-FOV images, bypassing the computationally intensive iterative sampling process of traditional diffusion models that begin from Gaussian noise. The authors demonstrate that I2SB achieves superior quantitative performance (RMSE, PSNR, SSIM) compared to other state-of-the-art diffusion models, including cDDPM and patch-based diffusion methods, while also being significantly faster at inference. The method's efficiency, with a single-step inference of 0.19 seconds per 2D slice, makes it suitable for real-time or clinical deployment. They validate the model on both simulated noisy data and real clinical head CT data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the application of Image-to-Image Schrodinger Bridge models to the CT FOV extension problem. While diffusion models have been used in CT image reconstruction, using the SB approach to directly learn the mapping between truncated and full FOV images is a valuable contribution. This addresses the limitations of standard diffusion models regarding computational efficiency and interpretability for this specific task. The paper clearly contrasts I2SB with existing diffusion methods like cDDPM and patchDiffusion, emphasizing the speed and structural integrity advantages.

* **Significance:**  The paper's significance stems from its potential to improve the clinical utility of CT imaging, especially in scenarios where data truncation is a common issue (e.g., radiation therapy planning, ROI imaging, or when large patients exceed the scanner's dimensions).  The demonstrated speed advantage of I2SB over other diffusion models is crucial for clinical applications where real-time reconstruction is desired. The quantitative results (RMSE reductions, PSNR/SSIM improvements) are compelling and support the claim of superior performance. The validation on both simulated and real data is also significant and strengthens the credibility of the approach.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Efficiency:** The primary strength is the significant speed improvement compared to other diffusion models, making it practically viable for clinical settings.
    * **Performance:** The I2SB model demonstrates superior quantitative results in terms of RMSE, PSNR, and SSIM, indicating accurate and consistent reconstructions.
    * **Direct Mapping:** The use of Schrodinger Bridge provides a direct interpretable mapping improving image fidelity
    * **Validation:** The thorough validation on both simulated noisy data and real clinical data enhances the reliability of the findings.
    * **Clear Explanation:** The paper provides a clear explanation of the I2SB approach and its advantages over other diffusion models.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Real Data:** While real data is included, a larger and more diverse real dataset would further strengthen the findings.
    * **Soft Tissue Boundaries:** The paper acknowledges the limitation in perfectly restoring soft-tissue detail, likely influenced by the WCE pre-processing and the assumption of water-equivalent attenuation. Future work could address this by training end-to-end or using different input features.
    * **Potential Domain Gap:** Acknowledging the domain gap between the simulated training data and the real CBCT test data raises a concern about the generalizability of the method to other CT systems or acquisition protocols.
    * **Implementation details:** Further details around network architecture choices could be useful for reproducibility.
    * **Ablation Studies:** While the paper compares different diffusion models, more detailed ablation studies, like analyzing the impact of different SB parameters would strengthen the contribution.

* **Overall Impact:** This work can significantly impact CT image reconstruction. The significant performance gain and improvement on reconstruction quality over the state-of-the-art will have a high impact in practical applications.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I assign a score of **8**.

Rationale:

* **High Score Aspects:** The practical speed advantage of I2SB and its clear improvement in quantitative metrics justify a high score. This makes I2SB potentially transformative for clinical CT imaging workflows. The good performance achieved on real data further reinforces this.

* **Deductions:**  The limitations of the real data, some questions about domain gap generalizability, and some missing implementation details, prevent it from achieving a score of 9 or 10. While SB models have been used in other image-to-image translation applications, its effective adaptation and optimization for the crucial clinical problem of CT FOV extension warrants significant recognition.  Addressing the limitations in future work could push the score higher.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 8/10

### ORFuzz: Fuzzing the "Other Side" of LLM Safety -- Testing Over-Refusal
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11222v1)
- **Authors**: Haonan Zhang, Dongxia Wang, Yi Liu, Kexin Chen, Jiashui Wang, Xinlei Ying, Long Liu, Wenhai Wang
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) increasingly exhibit over-refusal - erroneously rejecting benign queries due to overly conservative safety measures - a critical functional flaw that undermines their reliability and usability. Current methods for testing this behavior are demonstrably inadequate, suffering from flawed benchmarks and limited test generation capabilities, as highlighted by our empirical user study. To the best of our knowledge, this paper introduces the first evolutionary testing framework, ORFuzz, for the systematic detection and analysis of LLM over-refusals. ORFuzz uniquely integrates three core components: (1) safety category-aware seed selection for comprehensive test coverage, (2) adaptive mutator optimization using reasoning LLMs to generate effective test cases, and (3) OR-Judge, a human-aligned judge model validated to accurately reflect user perception of toxicity and refusal. Our extensive evaluations demonstrate that ORFuzz generates diverse, validated over-refusal instances at a rate (6.98% average) more than double that of leading baselines, effectively uncovering vulnerabilities. Furthermore, ORFuzz's outputs form the basis of ORFuzzSet, a new benchmark of 1,855 highly transferable test cases that achieves a superior 63.56% average over-refusal rate across 10 diverse LLMs, significantly outperforming existing datasets. ORFuzz and ORFuzzSet provide a robust automated testing framework and a valuable community resource, paving the way for developing more reliable and trustworthy LLM-based software systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "ORFUZZ: Fuzzing the 'Other Side' of LLM Safety – Testing Over-Refusal":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ORFUZZ, a novel evolutionary fuzzing framework designed to detect over-refusal behavior in Large Language Models (LLMs). Over-refusal occurs when an LLM erroneously rejects benign queries due to overly conservative safety measures. ORFUZZ integrates three key components: safety category-aware seed selection, adaptive mutator optimization (using reasoning LLMs), and OR-JUDGE, a human-aligned judge model for evaluating toxicity and refusal. The authors demonstrate that ORFUZZ generates more diverse and valid over-refusal instances than existing baselines. Furthermore, they create ORFUZZSET, a new benchmark dataset of transferable test cases, showcasing superior performance in triggering over-refusal across various LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper makes a significant contribution by addressing the often-overlooked issue of over-refusal in LLMs. While jailbreaking has received considerable attention, the systematic testing and mitigation of over-refusal is less explored. The integration of evolutionary fuzzing techniques, combined with safety category awareness and human-aligned evaluation, is novel. The design of mutators specialized for inducing over-refusal (rather than jailbreaking) is another plus.
*   **Significance:** Over-refusal has serious practical implications, impacting the usability and reliability of LLMs in real-world applications. By developing ORFUZZ, the authors provide a valuable tool for developers to identify and address this problem. The ORFUZZSET benchmark is also a crucial contribution, offering a standardized way to evaluate LLM robustness against over-refusal. The user study highlighting the flaws of existing over-refusal benchmarks adds further weight to the paper's claims.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The problem statement is clearly articulated and well-motivated.
    *   The design of ORFUZZ is well-explained, with a clear description of each component and their integration.
    *   The adaptive mutator optimization leveraging reasoning LLMs is a particularly interesting and effective technique.
    *   The human-aligned judge model (OR-JUDGE) addresses a critical limitation of existing approaches, ensuring that the evaluation of over-refusal aligns with human perceptions.
    *   The experimental evaluation is comprehensive, comparing ORFUZZ against relevant baselines and providing ablation studies to assess the contribution of individual components.
    *   The creation and validation of the ORFUZZSET benchmark enhances the reproducibility and comparability of future research in this area.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the paper demonstrates the effectiveness of ORFUZZ, it doesn't provide detailed insights into the specific types of safety mechanisms that are most susceptible to over-refusal. Understanding these mechanisms could inform the development of more targeted defenses.
    *   The performance of ORFUZZ is dependent on the quality of the reasoning LLM used for mutator optimization. The paper could benefit from a discussion on the sensitivity of ORFUZZ to the choice of reasoning LLM.
    *   Although the user study is valuable, the sample size is somewhat limited. A larger user study would provide more robust evidence for the validity of the findings.
    *   The user study should analyze what makes the existing datasets toxic beyond the intended "harmful" category so that the research community can learn about potential pitfalls when building benchmarks.

**Score:** 8

**Justification:** The paper makes a significant and novel contribution to the field of LLM safety by addressing the issue of over-refusal. The proposed framework, ORFUZZ, is well-designed, thoroughly evaluated, and demonstrably effective. The creation of the ORFUZZSET benchmark provides a valuable resource for the research community. While some aspects, such as detailed analysis of vulnerable safety mechanisms and scalability of the user study, could be further strengthened, the paper represents a substantial advance in the area. The potential impact on the development of more reliable and trustworthy LLMs is considerable.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 8/10

### Generalized Decoupled Learning for Enhancing Open-Vocabulary Dense Perception
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11256v1)
- **Authors**: Junjie Wang, Keyu Chen, Yulin Li, Bin Chen, Hengshuang Zhao, Xiaojuan Qi, Zhuotao Tian
- **Abstract**: Dense visual perception tasks have been constrained by their reliance on predefined categories, limiting their applicability in real-world scenarios where visual concepts are unbounded. While Vision-Language Models (VLMs) like CLIP have shown promise in open-vocabulary tasks, their direct application to dense perception often leads to suboptimal performance due to limitations in local feature representation. In this work, we present our observation that CLIP's image tokens struggle to effectively aggregate information from spatially or semantically related regions, resulting in features that lack local discriminability and spatial consistency. To address this issue, we propose DeCLIP, a novel framework that enhances CLIP by decoupling the self-attention module to obtain ``content'' and ``context'' features respectively. \revise{The context features are enhanced by jointly distilling semantic correlations from Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) and object integrity cues from diffusion models, thereby enhancing spatial consistency. In parallel, the content features are aligned with image crop representations and constrained by region correlations from VFMs to improve local discriminability. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DeCLIP establishes a solid foundation for open-vocabulary dense perception, consistently achieving state-of-the-art performance across a broad spectrum of tasks, including 2D detection and segmentation, 3D instance segmentation, video instance segmentation, and 6D object pose estimation.} Code is available at https://github.com/xiaomoguhz/DeCLIP
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Generalized Decoupled Learning for Enhancing Open-Vocabulary Dense Perception":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of improving open-vocabulary dense perception by enhancing the feature representations learned by Vision-Language Models (VLMs) like CLIP. The authors observe that directly applying CLIP to dense perception tasks suffers from suboptimal performance due to limitations in CLIP's ability to aggregate information from spatially or semantically related regions. To address this, they propose DeCLIP, a framework that decouples CLIP's self-attention module into "content" and "context" features. Context features are enhanced by distilling information from Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) and diffusion models to improve spatial consistency. Content features are aligned with image crop representations and constrained by region correlations from VFMs to improve local discriminability. The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of DeCLIP across various tasks, including 2D detection/segmentation, 3D instance segmentation, video instance segmentation, and 6D object pose estimation, consistently achieving state-of-the-art performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of decoupling the self-attention module within CLIP to separately optimize "content" and "context" features is a significant contribution. While other works have explored combining CLIP with VFMs, DeCLIP's specific approach to decoupling and independently enhancing these features distinguishes it. The use of diffusion models to improve semantic integrity of context features and the VFM constraints on content features are also novel. The analysis of CLIP's attention patterns, specifically the identification of "proxy tokens" and their interference with dense perception, provides valuable insight.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to unlock more effective open-vocabulary dense perception. The improvements demonstrated across a broad range of tasks highlight the generalizability and potential impact of DeCLIP. By improving the fundamental feature representations of CLIP, the work facilitates better performance for downstream tasks. The results position DeCLIP as a strong foundational model for open-vocabulary perception.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined Problem and Clear Motivation:** The paper clearly identifies a limitation of directly applying CLIP to dense perception and provides a strong motivation for addressing this issue.
    *   **Detailed Analysis:** The authors conduct a thorough analysis of CLIP's attention mechanisms, providing valuable insights into its shortcomings.
    *   **Novel Approach:** The decoupled learning strategy is a novel and effective approach to enhance CLIP's feature representations.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents extensive experimental results across various tasks and datasets, demonstrating the robustness and generalizability of DeCLIP.
    *   **State-of-the-Art Performance:** The consistently superior performance compared to existing methods highlights the effectiveness of the proposed approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** The DeCLIP framework involves multiple components (VFMs, diffusion models, distillation losses), which adds complexity. Further analysis of the trade-offs between these components would be valuable.
    *   **Computational Cost:** Although the fine-tuning is unsupervised, the use of VFMs and diffusion models likely increases the computational cost compared to simply using CLIP. The paper does not provide a thorough analysis of computational complexity.
    *   **Limited Qualitative Analysis:** While there are qualitative results for some tasks, more extensive visualizations of the enhanced content and context features would further strengthen the paper.
    *   **Incremental Innovation:** While novel, the work heavily builds upon existing models (CLIP, VFMs, diffusion models). It's a sophisticated *integration* of various methods, but the core breakthrough might be considered incremental by some.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has strong potential to influence the field of open-vocabulary perception. It provides a valuable framework for enhancing VLMs for dense prediction tasks. Other researchers can build upon DeCLIP to develop more effective open-vocabulary perception systems. The paper's insights into CLIP's attention mechanisms can also guide future research in this area.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution to the field of open-vocabulary dense perception by addressing a key limitation of CLIP and proposing a novel and effective solution. The extensive evaluation and state-of-the-art performance demonstrate the practical value of DeCLIP. While the method is complex and builds upon existing models, the specific approach to decoupling and enhancing CLIP's features is novel and significant. The paper is well-written, clearly explains the proposed method, and provides sufficient experimental results to support its claims. Taking into account all the strengths and weaknesses, I would assign this paper a score of **8**. It's a very strong paper with a high level of technical sophistication, innovative ideas, and significant impact on a relevant problem.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Hallucination in LLM-Based Code Generation: An Automotive Case Study
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11257v1)
- **Authors**: Marc Pavel, Nenad Petrovic, Lukasz Mazur, Vahid Zolfaghari, Fengjunjie Pan, Alois Knoll
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown significant potential in automating code generation tasks offering new opportunities across software engineering domains. However, their practical application remains limited due to hallucinations - outputs that appear plausible but are factually incorrect, unverifiable or nonsensical. This paper investigates hallucination phenomena in the context of code generation with a specific focus on the automotive domain. A case study is presented that evaluates multiple code LLMs for three different prompting complexities ranging from a minimal one-liner prompt to a prompt with Covesa Vehicle Signal Specifications (VSS) as additional context and finally to a prompt with an additional code skeleton. The evaluation reveals a high frequency of syntax violations, invalid reference errors and API knowledge conflicts in state-of-the-art models GPT-4.1, Codex and GPT-4o. Among the evaluated models, only GPT-4.1 and GPT-4o were able to produce a correct solution when given the most context-rich prompt. Simpler prompting strategies failed to yield a working result, even after multiple refinement iterations. These findings highlight the need for effective mitigation techniques to ensure the safe and reliable use of LLM generated code, especially in safety-critical domains such as automotive software systems.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Hallucination in LLM-Based Code Generation: An Automotive Case Study":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the phenomenon of hallucinations in Large Language Models (LLMs) when generating code, specifically within the automotive domain. The authors perform a case study where they task several code LLMs (GPT-4.1, Codex, GPT-4o, and others) with generating Python code to turn off windshield wipers when a car's hood is open, using COVESA Vehicle Signal Specification (VSS) signals. They test three prompting strategies: a minimal baseline prompt, a signal-augmented prompt (including a list of relevant VSS signals), and a template-augmented prompt (including a code skeleton). The evaluation focuses on identifying different types of hallucinations, such as syntax errors, invalid references, API knowledge conflicts, and the use of non-existent VSS signals. They also implement an iterative repair strategy based on static analysis and runtime errors to mitigate hallucinations. The results indicate that more complex prompts generally lead to better initial code quality and that while the repair strategy improves the code, complete solutions are only reliably achieved with the template-augmented prompt and specific LLMs (GPT-4.1 and GPT-4o).

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    * **Relevant Problem:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in the practical application of LLMs for code generation, specifically the issue of hallucinations which can be especially dangerous in safety-critical domains like automotive.
    * **Clear Case Study:** The case study is well-defined, using a realistic automotive scenario with clearly defined requirements and VSS signals.
    * **Systematic Evaluation:** The authors use a structured approach to evaluate the performance of different LLMs with varying prompting strategies and a defined heuristic scoring system. This provides a quantifiable measure of the LLMs' success.
    * **Hallucination Taxonomy:** The paper uses a well-defined taxonomy of code hallucinations, which helps to categorize and analyze the types of errors that occur.
    * **Mitigation Strategy:** The implementation of an iterative repair strategy provides a practical approach for mitigating hallucinations in generated code.
    * **Focus on an Unfamiliar Domain:** Testing within a domain not commonly present in standard LLM training datasets (automotive with Covesa VSS) highlights the limitations of current LLMs.
* **Weaknesses:**

    * **Limited Scope:**  The case study is confined to a single, relatively simple task.  While informative, the generalizability of the findings to more complex automotive software tasks is limited.
    * **Offline LLMs:** While some online LLMs were included, many of the tested models were offline code generation tools. Results are less helpful in practical situations where online options are the most convenient.
    * **Heuristic Scoring:** The scoring metric is heuristic, and although it's explained, its subjectivity could influence the results. The weights for the penalties seem arbitrary.
    * **Limited Mitigation Depth:** The study primarily focuses on a single iterative repair technique, which, while effective to some extent, may not be the most optimal or adaptable solution for all hallucination types.
    * **Lack of In-Depth Error Analysis:**  While the paper identifies different hallucination types, it could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the *underlying causes* of these hallucinations in each model. Why, specifically, is *this* model hallucinating about *this* particular signal?
    * **Novelty Questionable:** The methodology is standard (testing LLMs with varying prompts and post-processing). The specific application to an automotive task and VSS is somewhat novel, but the core approach isn't groundbreaking. Iterative repair is also not a new idea.

* **Novelty and Significance:** The paper makes a contribution by specifically investigating the issue of hallucinations in the context of automotive code generation.  The use of COVESA VSS adds a level of realism and domain-specificity that is not often seen in LLM evaluation studies. It highlights the challenges of using LLMs in safety-critical domains and underscores the need for more robust mitigation techniques.  However, the paper's methodology and mitigation strategy are not particularly novel. It essentially applies existing techniques to a new application area.

**Justification of Score:**

I am assigning a score of **6** to this paper.  While the paper presents a relevant and well-executed case study, it lacks significant novelty in terms of methodology or mitigation strategy. The specific application to automotive and the use of COVESA VSS is a strength, making it somewhat relevant for researchers and practitioners working in the automotive software domain. The results are interesting and highlight the challenges involved.  However, the paper is more of a practical demonstration of known problems and existing (though refined) solutions rather than a major advancement in the field. The use of a heuristic scoring system and a limited scope weaken the impact, and deeper error analysis would substantially strengthen it. A higher score would require a more groundbreaking contribution or a more comprehensive analysis of the underlying causes of hallucinations.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 6/10

### Group Fairness Meets the Black Box: Enabling Fair Algorithms on Closed LLMs via Post-Processing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11258v1)
- **Authors**: Ruicheng Xian, Yuxuan Wan, Han Zhao
- **Abstract**: Instruction fine-tuned large language models (LLMs) enable a simple zero-shot or few-shot prompting paradigm, also known as in-context learning, for building prediction models. This convenience, combined with continued advances in LLM capability, has the potential to drive their adoption across a broad range of domains, including high-stakes applications where group fairness -- preventing disparate impacts across demographic groups -- is essential. The majority of existing approaches to enforcing group fairness on LLM-based classifiers rely on traditional fair algorithms applied via model fine-tuning or head-tuning on final-layer embeddings, but they are no longer applicable to closed-weight LLMs under the in-context learning setting, which include some of the most capable commercial models today, such as GPT-4, Gemini, and Claude. In this paper, we propose a framework for deriving fair classifiers from closed-weight LLMs via prompting: the LLM is treated as a feature extractor, and features are elicited from its probabilistic predictions (e.g., token log probabilities) using prompts strategically designed for the specified fairness criterion to obtain sufficient statistics for fair classification; a fair algorithm is then applied to these features to train a lightweight fair classifier in a post-hoc manner. Experiments on five datasets, including three tabular ones, demonstrate strong accuracy-fairness tradeoffs for the classifiers derived by our framework from both open-weight and closed-weight LLMs; in particular, our framework is data-efficient and outperforms fair classifiers trained on LLM embeddings (i.e., head-tuning) or from scratch on raw tabular features.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of enforcing group fairness on classification models built using closed-weight Large Language Models (LLMs) like GPT-4, Gemini, and Claude. These LLMs, unlike open-weight LLMs, don't allow direct access to model weights or internal representations, making traditional fairness algorithms (which rely on fine-tuning or embedding manipulation) inapplicable. The paper proposes a novel framework where the closed-weight LLM acts as a feature extractor.  The LLM's probabilistic predictions are elicited using strategically designed prompts based on the task at hand and the desired fairness criterion. These extracted features are then used to train a lightweight, post-hoc fair classifier.  The framework is evaluated on five datasets (including tabular ones) and demonstrated strong accuracy-fairness tradeoffs, with particular data efficiency advantages in low-data regimes, outperforming fair classifiers trained on LLM embeddings or from scratch on raw tabular features.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its adaptation of feature extraction for fairness in the context of *closed-weight* LLMs.  While the idea of using LLMs as feature extractors isn't entirely new, the specific application to fairness in scenarios where model access is limited and the strategic design of prompts to extract sufficient statistics for fairness considerations is a significant contribution.
*   **Significance:** The work addresses a critical and growing problem. As LLMs are increasingly deployed in high-stakes domains, ensuring fairness becomes paramount. The method's applicability to closed-weight LLMs is a major advantage, as many of the most powerful commercial LLMs fall into this category. The data efficiency is significant as well, especially given the high cost of acquiring labeled data for high-stakes tasks. The ability to use and enforce fairness on closed model API's could have a large impact because it's accessible, and provides some transparency.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   The problem formulation is relevant and timely.
    *   The proposed framework is well-motivated, theoretically sound, and practically applicable to real-world scenarios using API-accessible LLMs.
    *   The empirical evaluation is comprehensive, spanning various datasets, fairness criteria, LLMs, and fair algorithms.
    *   The comparison to alternative approaches (training on embeddings, from scratch) provides valuable insights into the method's strengths and weaknesses.
    *   The ablation study exploring the necessity of prompting for fairness-related variables strengthens the theoretical underpinnings.
    *   The data efficiency finding (outperforming embeddings in low-data regimes) is particularly impactful.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on prompt engineering is a potential weakness. Although the re-fitting step mitigates the prompt phrasing, sensitivity is present for zero-shot tasks. Also there is significant time-spend in prompt enigneering for effective performance.
    *   While the paper demonstrates the framework's effectiveness, it doesn't delve deeply into the theoretical properties of the extracted features. Understanding what information is preserved (and discarded) during the feature extraction process could further refine the method and improve its performance.
    *   The framework's reliance on Bayes-optimal LLM predictions might not hold true in every scenario, as it is unlikely in practice. In such cases, there could be additional information to elicit from the LLM that may improve the performance of the subsequent fair algorithm.
    *   The study primarily focuses on classification tasks. Extending the approach to other tasks, such as regression or ranking, would further broaden its applicability.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of fair machine learning, particularly in the context of LLMs.  It offers a practical and accessible solution for ensuring fairness in scenarios where direct model access is not available. The data efficiency advantages make it particularly appealing for resource-constrained settings. Future research can build upon this work by exploring more sophisticated feature extraction techniques, theoretically analyzing the properties of the extracted features, and extending the approach to other tasks.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:**

The paper presents a significant and novel contribution to fair machine learning by addressing the practical problem of enforcing fairness on closed-weight LLMs. Its strengths in problem formulation, methodology, empirical validation, and potential impact outweigh its weaknesses related to prompt engineering and limited theoretical analysis. The data efficiency advantages and applicability to real-world scenarios make it a valuable contribution, justifying a score of 7.5. While the approach is not a complete game changer (it still depends on the underlying LLM quality and requires careful prompt engineering), it provides a crucial and practical solution for a growing problem. The 7.5 score acknowledges the significant practical value and solid empirical results, tempered by the identified limitations.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### UNVEILING: What Makes Linguistics Olympiad Puzzles Tricky for LLMs?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11260v1)
- **Authors**: Mukund Choudhary, KV Aditya Srivatsa, Gaurja Aeron, Antara Raaghavi Bhattacharya, Dang Khoa Dang Dinh, Ikhlasul Akmal Hanif, Daria Kotova, Ekaterina Kochmar, Monojit Choudhury
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated potential in reasoning tasks, but their performance on linguistics puzzles remains consistently poor. These puzzles, often derived from Linguistics Olympiad (LO) contests, provide a minimal contamination environment to assess LLMs' linguistic reasoning abilities across low-resource languages. This work analyses LLMs' performance on 629 problems across 41 low-resource languages by labelling each with linguistically informed features to unveil weaknesses. Our analyses show that LLMs struggle with puzzles involving higher morphological complexity and perform better on puzzles involving linguistic features that are also found in English. We also show that splitting words into morphemes as a pre-processing step improves solvability, indicating a need for more informed and language-specific tokenisers. These findings thus offer insights into some challenges in linguistic reasoning and modelling of low-resource languages.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the challenges Large Language Models (LLMs) face when solving Linguistics Olympiad (LO) puzzles, particularly those involving low-resource languages (LRLs). The authors analyze the performance of various LLMs on a dataset of 629 problems from 41 LRLs, annotating puzzles with linguistic features derived from the World Atlas of Language Structures (WALS) and other relevant attributes. Their analysis reveals that LLMs struggle with puzzles involving high morphological complexity and perform better on puzzles containing linguistic features similar to those found in English. They also show that explicitly separating words into morphemes improves LLM performance, suggesting a need for language-specific tokenization strategies. The paper concludes by discussing implications for LRL learning and metalinguistic reasoning in LLMs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main contribution lies in its fine-grained, feature-level analysis of LLM performance on LO puzzles. While previous studies have noted LLMs' poor performance on such puzzles, this work goes beyond simply demonstrating the issue. By annotating puzzles with linguistic features and correlating them with performance, the authors provide a more nuanced understanding of *why* LLMs struggle. The observation that morpheme splitting improves performance, especially for morphologically rich languages, is a valuable insight.

*   **Significance:** The findings have several significant implications for the field:

    *   **Linguistic Reasoning:** The paper sheds light on the limitations of current LLMs in performing linguistic reasoning, especially in LRLs. It highlights the need for models that can better handle morphological complexity and adapt to diverse linguistic structures.

    *   **Tokenization:** The results underscore the importance of language-specific tokenization strategies for LRLs. The study suggests that current tokenization methods, often trained on high-resource languages, may not be optimal for morphologically rich languages. This is a major bottleneck to applying LLMs in LRLs.

    *   **Low-Resource Language Modeling:** The study provides valuable insights for improving LLM-based research on sample-efficient LRL learning. By identifying specific linguistic features that pose challenges to LLMs, researchers can develop more targeted training methods.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Rigorous methodology:** The paper employs a well-defined annotation scheme based on established linguistic frameworks like WALS. The inter-annotator agreement is good, which ensures data reliability.
    *   **Comprehensive analysis:** The paper analyzes a diverse set of LLMs, including both open-source and closed-source models. This allows for a more comprehensive assessment of their capabilities and limitations.
    *   **Actionable insights:** The paper offers specific, actionable insights for improving LLM performance on LRLs, such as the use of morpheme splitting and language-specific tokenization.
    *   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, with clear explanations of the methodology and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited data size:** While the dataset of 629 problems is reasonable, a larger dataset could provide even more robust statistical findings and generalisations. Also, only 64 puzzles were linguistically tagged.
    *   **Focus on specific puzzle type:** The study focuses on Rosetta Stone-style puzzles, which may not be representative of all types of linguistic reasoning tasks.
    *   **Potential for model update:** LLMs, especially those in development, are constantly changing. The versions used in this paper may soon be outdated, potentially affecting the generalisability of some findings.

*   **Impact and Future Directions:** The paper's findings are likely to influence future research on LRL modeling and metalinguistic reasoning in LLMs. It is likely that this paper will spur future research into better tokenisation strategies. The study will inform development of better targeted training methods.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper represents a valuable contribution to the field. It makes a solid advance in our understanding of LLM limitations in linguistic reasoning.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Inference performance evaluation for LLMs on edge devices with a novel benchmarking framework and metric
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11269v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Chen, Cong Tian, Zixuan He, Bin Yu, Yepang Liu, Jialun Cao
- **Abstract**: With the significant success achieved by large language models (LLMs) like LLaMA, edge computing-based LLM inference services for mobile and PC are in high demand for data privacy. However, different edge platforms have different hardware characteristics and the large demand for memory capacity and bandwidth makes it very challenging to deploy and benchmark LLMs on edge devices. In this paper, we introduce a benchmarking tool named ELIB (edge LLM inference benchmarking) to evaluate LLM inference performance of different edge platforms, and propose a novel metric named MBU to indicate the percentage of the theoretically efficient use of available memory bandwidth for a specific model running on edge hardware to optimize memory usage. We deploy ELIB on three edge platforms and benchmark using five quantized models to optimize MBU in combination with other metrics such as FLOPS, throughput, latency and accuracy. And we analyze the results to derive the key factors, constraints, unpredictability in optimizing MBU that can guide deploying LLMs on more edge platforms.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ELIB (edge LLM inference benchmarking), a new benchmarking tool and framework for evaluating the inference performance of large language models (LLMs) on various edge computing platforms. It proposes a novel metric called MBU (Model Bandwidth Utilization) to quantify the efficient use of available memory bandwidth during LLM inference. The authors deploy ELIB on three edge platforms (NanoPI, Xiaomi Redmi Note12 Turbo, and MacBook Air 2022) and benchmark five quantized LLaMA models. The results are analyzed to identify key factors, constraints, and unpredictability in optimizing MBU, aiming to provide guidance for deploying LLMs on edge devices.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**

    *   **Addresses a relevant and timely problem:** Deploying and benchmarking LLMs on edge devices is a growing area of interest due to privacy concerns and the need for low-latency applications. The paper directly tackles the challenges associated with this task.
    *   **Introduction of a novel metric:** The MBU metric is a valuable contribution. It provides a specific way to understand and optimize memory bandwidth utilization, which is crucial for efficient LLM inference on resource-constrained edge devices. This goes beyond traditional metrics like FLOPS and latency.
    *   **Comprehensive benchmarking framework:** ELIB seems like a well-designed tool that simplifies the deployment and evaluation process across different edge platforms, especially hybrid ones.  The Model-Graph-Kernel structure looks elegant.
    *   **Practical experimentation:** The paper includes experiments on a diverse set of edge devices and with different quantization models. This strengthens the practical relevance of the findings.
    *   **Analysis and Heuristics:** The paper attempts to derive some heuristics and key factors to guide LLM deployment. This is a valuable step in making the results more practically applicable.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Tooling:** While ELIB seems useful, the components are mostly based on llama.cpp. The primary novelty is the automation, integration, and the addition of the MBU metric and analysis. The actual framework changes are less novel.
    *   **Quantization Choices:** While justified by using llama.cpp, focusing solely on GGML-based quantization limits the scope.  Other quantization techniques and libraries (e.g., from MLC-LLM, TensorRT) might offer different trade-offs.
    *   **Generalizability of the Heuristics:** While the identified factors (batch size, sequence length, KV cache management) are relevant, the specific guidance or "heuristics" derived are not very surprising and are often device and model specific, as well as quantization aware. A more robust, quantitatively validated set of guidelines would be stronger.
    *   **Accuracy concerns with GPU/OpenCL**: The large accuracy drops on the Xiaomi phone and NanoPI when using OpenCL/GPU are a major limitation. This suggests issues with the OpenCL implementations or data precision on these devices, potentially undermining any performance improvements they achieve.
    *   **Threats to Validity could be more robust**: The paper acknowledges the issues with generalizability, but doesn't do enough to try to mitigate the validity of the edge devices.

*   **Significance and Impact:**

    *   The paper contributes to the growing body of research on edge-based LLM inference, particularly by highlighting the importance of memory bandwidth utilization. The MBU metric may influence future benchmarking efforts.
    *   The ELIB framework could be adopted by researchers and developers in the field to streamline their evaluation process.
    *   The practical insights and guidelines, although not groundbreaking, can inform the design and deployment of LLMs on edge devices.

*   **Overall:** The paper is a solid contribution, but it has limitations that impact its overall novelty and significance. The introduction of the MBU metric and the automation of the benchmarking process with ELIB are valuable, but accuracy concerns and questionable generalizability significantly lower the impact of the paper.

**Score: 6.5**

**Justification:**

A score of 6.5 is given because:

*   The paper addresses a timely and relevant problem and proposes a useful metric (MBU) which constitutes a clear contribution.
*   However, much of the framework is based on existing work (llama.cpp), and the conclusions are of limited surprise and often highly specific.
*   The significant accuracy issues identified with OpenCL implementations on certain devices raise questions about the reliability of the entire study.
*   The lack of more robust validation and detailed GPU implementations lowers confidence in the results and limits the impact. The experiments, although present, lack an important aspect: thorough implementation analysis (for the GPU side in particular), causing the study to be inconclusive.

- **Classification**: cs.PF
- **Score**: 6/10

### Enhancing Supervised Composed Image Retrieval via Reasoning-Augmented Representation Engineering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11272v1)
- **Authors**: Jun Li, Kai Li, Shaoguo Liu, Tingting Gao
- **Abstract**: Composed Image Retrieval (CIR) presents a significant challenge as it requires jointly understanding a reference image and a modified textual instruction to find relevant target images. Some existing methods attempt to use a two-stage approach to further refine retrieval results. However, this often requires additional training of a ranking model. Despite the success of Chain-of-Thought (CoT) techniques in reducing training costs for language models, their application in CIR tasks remains limited -- compressing visual information into text or relying on elaborate prompt designs. Besides, existing works only utilize it for zero-shot CIR, as it is challenging to achieve satisfactory results in supervised CIR with a well-trained model. In this work, we proposed a framework that includes the Pyramid Matching Model with Training-Free Refinement (PMTFR) to address these challenges. Through a simple but effective module called Pyramid Patcher, we enhanced the Pyramid Matching Model's understanding of visual information at different granularities. Inspired by representation engineering, we extracted representations from COT data and injected them into the LVLMs. This approach allowed us to obtain refined retrieval scores in the Training-Free Refinement paradigm without relying on explicit textual reasoning, further enhancing performance. Extensive experiments on CIR benchmarks demonstrate that PMTFR surpasses state-of-the-art methods in supervised CIR tasks. The code will be made public.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new framework called PMTFR (Pyramid Matching Model with Training-Free Refinement) for Composed Image Retrieval (CIR).  PMTFR enhances the understanding of visual information by incorporating a Pyramid Patcher module, which provides the model with visual tokens of varying receptive fields.  Furthermore, the paper proposes a training-free refinement paradigm.
