- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reinforcing Video Reasoning Segmentation to Think Before It Segments
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11538v1)
- **Authors**: Sitong Gong, Lu Zhang, Yunzhi Zhuge, Xu Jia, Pingping Zhang, Huchuan Lu
- **Abstract**: Video reasoning segmentation (VRS) endeavors to delineate referred objects in videos guided by implicit instructions that encapsulate human intent and temporal logic. Previous approaches leverage large vision language models (LVLMs) to encode object semantics into <SEG> tokens for mask prediction. However, this paradigm suffers from limited interpretability during inference and suboptimal performance due to inadequate spatiotemporal reasoning. Drawing inspiration from seminal breakthroughs in reinforcement learning, we introduce Veason-R1, a specialized LVLM for VRS that emphasizes structured reasoning in segmentation. Veason-R1 is trained through Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) augmented with Chain-of-Thought (CoT) initialization. To begin with, we curate high-quality CoT training data to instill structured reasoning trajectories, bridging video-level semantics and frame-level spatial grounding, yielding the supervised fine-tuned model Veason-SFT. Subsequently, GRPO fine-tuning encourages efficient exploration of the reasoning space by optimizing reasoning chains. To this end, we incorporate a holistic reward mechanism that synergistically enhances spatial alignment and temporal consistency, bolstering keyframe localization and fine-grained grounding. Comprehensive empirical evaluations demonstrate that Veason-R1 achieves state-of-the-art performance on multiple benchmarks, surpassing prior art by significant margins (e.g., +1.3 J &F in ReVOS and +10.0 J &F in ReasonVOS), while exhibiting robustness to hallucinations (+8.8 R). Our code and model weights will be available at Veason-R1.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Veason-R1, a novel approach to Video Reasoning Segmentation (VRS) that leverages reinforcement learning (RL) to improve the interpretability and spatiotemporal reasoning capabilities of Large Vision Language Models (LVLMs).  Veason-R1 is trained using Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) initialized with Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning.  The approach involves a two-stage training process: first, supervised fine-tuning (SFT) on a curated CoT dataset to instill structured reasoning, followed by GRPO fine-tuning to encourage efficient exploration of the reasoning space. The GRPO stage uses a holistic reward mechanism to enhance spatial alignment and temporal consistency.  Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on multiple VRS benchmarks, with improved robustness to hallucinations.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the application of reinforcement learning, specifically GRPO with CoT initialization, to the VRS task.  Previous VRS methods relied heavily on large-scale supervised fine-tuning of LVLMs using specialized tokens, which lacked explicit reasoning steps. The CoT initialization,  combined with the GRPO fine-tuning,  allows the model to learn structured reasoning trajectories, making the decision-making process more interpretable and efficient. The curated CoT dataset is another valuable contribution, although it builds upon existing CoT concepts. The explicit decomposition of the task into keyframe identification and spatial grounding is also a welcome approach for enhanced explainability.
*   **Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the substantial performance gains achieved on standard VRS benchmarks.  The improvements over prior state-of-the-art methods are particularly noteworthy on datasets requiring more complex reasoning and handling of temporal dynamics. The demonstrated robustness to hallucinations is another important advantage, as this is a common problem with LVLM-based approaches. The improved data efficiency (using fewer training examples than previous methods) is another important aspect. The use of GRPO instead of traditional RL is also significant as it reduces the need for a separate value function, simplifying the training process.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined and implemented RL framework for VRS.
    *   Significant performance improvements on multiple benchmarks.
    *   Demonstrated robustness to hallucinations.
    *   Improved data efficiency.
    *   Provides CoT samples and analysis to support reasoning.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While GRPO provides benefits, it is not a brand new method. It builds upon previous work, thus its novelty is incremental rather than revolutionary.
    *   Limited analysis of computational cost and training time. While data efficiency is mentioned, a more detailed comparison of computational resources would be beneficial.
    *   The reliance on SAM2 for unified consistency reward introduces a potential dependency on a pre-trained model, potentially limiting generalizability if SAM2 has limitations in certain video scenarios.
    *   The description of the implementation details could be more thorough for reproducibility.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in VRS by demonstrating the effectiveness of RL-based approaches for enhancing reasoning capabilities. The CoT-GRPO training paradigm could be adopted in other areas where structured reasoning and interpretability are crucial, such as robotic manipulation and autonomous driving.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of Video Reasoning Segmentation.  The application of GRPO with CoT,  the performance improvements,  and the enhanced robustness to hallucinations justify a high score. However, the incremental nature of the GRPO method and the dependence on SAM2 mean it falls short of being groundbreaking. There is also a need for further, more thorough detail to improve reproducibility. Therefore, a score of 8 is assigned, reflecting the significant improvements and potential influence of the work while acknowledging its limitations.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Copyright Protection for Large Language Models: A Survey of Methods, Challenges, and Trends
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11548v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenhua Xu, Xubin Yue, Zhebo Wang, Qichen Liu, Xixiang Zhao, Jingxuan Zhang, Wenjun Zeng, Wengpeng Xing, Dezhang Kong, Changting Lin, Meng Han
- **Abstract**: Copyright protection for large language models is of critical importance, given their substantial development costs, proprietary value, and potential for misuse. Existing surveys have predominantly focused on techniques for tracing LLM-generated content-namely, text watermarking-while a systematic exploration of methods for protecting the models themselves (i.e., model watermarking and model fingerprinting) remains absent. Moreover, the relationships and distinctions among text watermarking, model watermarking, and model fingerprinting have not been comprehensively clarified. This work presents a comprehensive survey of the current state of LLM copyright protection technologies, with a focus on model fingerprinting, covering the following aspects: (1) clarifying the conceptual connection from text watermarking to model watermarking and fingerprinting, and adopting a unified terminology that incorporates model watermarking into the broader fingerprinting framework; (2) providing an overview and comparison of diverse text watermarking techniques, highlighting cases where such methods can function as model fingerprinting; (3) systematically categorizing and comparing existing model fingerprinting approaches for LLM copyright protection; (4) presenting, for the first time, techniques for fingerprint transfer and fingerprint removal; (5) summarizing evaluation metrics for model fingerprints, including effectiveness, harmlessness, robustness, stealthiness, and reliability; and (6) discussing open challenges and future research directions. This survey aims to offer researchers a thorough understanding of both text watermarking and model fingerprinting technologies in the era of LLMs, thereby fostering further advances in protecting their intellectual property.
- **Summary**: This paper provides a comprehensive survey of copyright protection techniques for Large Language Models (LLMs), focusing on both text watermarking and model fingerprinting. It clarifies the conceptual connections between these techniques, categorizes model fingerprinting approaches (intrinsic and invasive), discusses fingerprint transfer and removal, summarizes evaluation metrics, and outlines open challenges and future research directions. The survey aims to offer researchers a thorough understanding of copyright protection in the LLM era.

**Evaluation of Novelty and Significance:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution by providing a much-needed systematic overview of the largely unexplored area of *model fingerprinting* for LLMs. Existing surveys have primarily focused on text watermarking, leaving a significant gap in the understanding of techniques to protect the models themselves. This survey addresses this gap and clarifies the often-blurred terminology surrounding text watermarking, model watermarking, and model fingerprinting.

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensive Coverage:** The survey covers a broad range of techniques and aspects, including fingerprint transfer and removal, which are often overlooked.
*   **Clear Taxonomy:** The classification of model fingerprinting approaches into intrinsic and invasive is helpful for understanding the diverse methods.
*   **Emphasis on Open Challenges:** The discussion of open challenges and future research directions provides a valuable roadmap for researchers in the field.
*   **Rigorous Definitions and Terminology:** Clarifying terminology is important for advancing the field and ensuring consistency across research efforts.
*   **Practical Considerations:** The inclusion of evaluation metrics (effectiveness, harmlessness, robustness, etc.) is crucial for practical application of these techniques.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Depth on Novelty of Specific Techniques:** The survey is broad but necessarily provides less depth on the specific innovations within each referenced paper. It focuses more on categorizing and summarizing than deeply analyzing the originality of the underlying techniques.
*   **Lack of Extensive Empirical Evaluation Comparison:** While the paper proposes evaluation metrics, it doesn't present a comparative empirical study of the techniques discussed. Such an evaluation would greatly strengthen the survey's impact.
*   **Future Trends are somewhat high-level:** The identified future trends could be made more concrete and actionable by linking them to specific emerging technical approaches.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper's contribution lies primarily in its organizational and explanatory power. It does a solid job of synthesizing diverse strands of work into a coherent overview of LLM copyright protection. While not presenting a specific novel technique *itself*, it significantly advances the field by identifying key aspects (fingerprint transfer, removal), providing a useful taxonomy, and pointing out critical open research questions.

The lack of empirical comparison and the relatively high-level discussion of future research limit its score. Also, while model fingerprinting techniques *exist*, the demonstrated efficacy of these techniques in the wild and the degree to which they are actually used by model creators remains largely unknown.

Therefore, while the survey is timely and addresses a critical need for a better understanding of LLM copyright protection, the limitations regarding in-depth analysis, empirical comparison, and the relatively underdeveloped state of the field it surveys justify a rating that reflects the current "promising but not yet realized" nature of the subject.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### Training-Free Anomaly Generation via Dual-Attention Enhancement in Diffusion Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11550v1)
- **Authors**: Zuo Zuo, Jiahao Dong, Yanyun Qu, Zongze Wu
- **Abstract**: Industrial anomaly detection (AD) plays a significant role in manufacturing where a long-standing challenge is data scarcity. A growing body of works have emerged to address insufficient anomaly data via anomaly generation. However, these anomaly generation methods suffer from lack of fidelity or need to be trained with extra data. To this end, we propose a training-free anomaly generation framework dubbed AAG, which is based on Stable Diffusion (SD)'s strong generation ability for effective anomaly image generation. Given a normal image, mask and a simple text prompt, AAG can generate realistic and natural anomalies in the specific regions and simultaneously keep contents in other regions unchanged. In particular, we propose Cross-Attention Enhancement (CAE) to re-engineer the cross-attention mechanism within Stable Diffusion based on the given mask. CAE increases the similarity between visual tokens in specific regions and text embeddings, which guides these generated visual tokens in accordance with the text description. Besides, generated anomalies need to be more natural and plausible with object in given image. We propose Self-Attention Enhancement (SAE) which improves similarity between each normal visual token and anomaly visual tokens. SAE ensures that generated anomalies are coherent with original pattern. Extensive experiments on MVTec AD and VisA datasets demonstrate effectiveness of AAG in anomaly generation and its utility. Furthermore, anomaly images generated by AAG can bolster performance of various downstream anomaly inspection tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a training-free anomaly generation framework called AAG (Anomaly-Agnostic Generation) to address the data scarcity problem in industrial anomaly detection (IAD). AAG leverages the generative power of Stable Diffusion (SD) without requiring additional training or knowledge of specific anomaly types. The approach takes a normal image, a mask indicating the region where an anomaly should be generated, and a simple text prompt as input. The core of AAG lies in two key modules: Cross-Attention Enhancement (CAE) and Self-Attention Enhancement (SAE). CAE modifies the cross-attention mechanism in SD to enhance the similarity between visual tokens in the masked region and the text prompt, guiding anomaly generation. SAE re-weights the self-attention mechanism to ensure the generated anomaly is coherent with the surrounding normal content. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of AAG on MVTec AD and VisA datasets, showing improved anomaly generation quality and a positive impact on downstream anomaly inspection tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using a pre-trained text-to-image diffusion model for anomaly generation is interesting but not entirely novel, as previous works like AnomalyDiffusion explore similar concepts. The **main novelty** lies in the specific design of the CAE and SAE modules, which aim to improve anomaly fidelity and coherence without requiring training data. The re-engineering of attention mechanisms within Stable Diffusion for this specific application is a valuable contribution. The training-free aspect, while appealing, also restricts the controllability of the anomalies and its applicability on specialized data types in IAD.

*   **Significance:** Addressing data scarcity in IAD is a very important problem. A training-free approach offers practical advantages in industrial settings where collecting anomaly data is expensive or impossible. The paper's experimental results demonstrate that AAG can generate higher-quality anomalies than existing cut-and-paste methods.  This can lead to better-performing anomaly detection systems. However, it is important to note that this generative quality is purely based on the subjective qualities of anomaly appearances, which is not well suited for safety-critical applications.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Training-Free:** A significant advantage for practical deployment.
    *   **Agnostic to Anomaly Type:** Can generate a variety of anomalies without specific training.
    *   **Improved Anomaly Quality:** Demonstrably better than traditional methods, as seen in the generated examples and quantitative results.
    *   **Positive Impact on Downstream Tasks:** Improves the performance of anomaly detection models.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Controllability:** Being training-free comes at the expense of fine-grained control over the generated anomalies. The range and types of anomalies that can be generated is limited by the priors learned by the diffusion model. The range and types of anomalies are constrained in specialized tasks of IAD, thus this framework might not be suitable for specialized data types in IAD.
    *   **Dependency on SD Performance:** The framework is inherently limited by the capabilities and biases of the pre-trained Stable Diffusion model.
    *   **Subjective Anomaly quality:** The quality assessment is predominantly based on metrics and visual inspection that may not be aligned with the specific requirements of different IAD applications.
    *   **Limited Evaluation:** Evaluation primarily on standard datasets.  More rigorous testing in real-world industrial scenarios would strengthen the paper.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the direction of anomaly generation research, particularly by highlighting the benefits of training-free approaches. Other researchers may explore similar attention-based manipulation techniques to further improve the controllability and fidelity of generated anomalies. The ease of use of this framework is attractive for many IAD tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the paper's novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I would assign it a score of **7**. The paper presents a valuable contribution by introducing a practical, training-free approach to anomaly generation. The CAE and SAE modules are well-designed and effectively improve anomaly quality and coherence. However, the limitations in controllability, dependence on SD's performance, and the need for more real-world validation prevent it from achieving a higher score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Aware First, Think Less: Dynamic Boundary Self-Awareness Drives Extreme Reasoning Efficiency in Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11582v1)
- **Authors**: Qiguang Chen, Dengyun Peng, Jinhao Liu, HuiKang Su, Jiannan Guan, Libo Qin, Wanxiang Che
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in large language models (LLMs) have greatly improved their capabilities on complex reasoning tasks through Long Chain-of-Thought (CoT). However, this approach often results in substantial redundancy, impairing computational efficiency and causing significant delays in real-time applications. To improve the efficiency, current methods often rely on human-defined difficulty priors, which do not align with the LLM's self-awared difficulty, leading to inefficiencies. In this paper, we introduce the Dynamic Reasoning-Boundary Self-Awareness Framework (DR. SAF), which enables models to dynamically assess and adjust their reasoning depth in response to problem complexity. DR. SAF integrates three key components: Boundary Self-Awareness Alignment, Adaptive Reward Management, and a Boundary Preservation Mechanism. These components allow models to optimize their reasoning processes, balancing efficiency and accuracy without compromising performance. Our experimental results demonstrate that DR. SAF achieves a 49.27% reduction in total response tokens with minimal loss in accuracy. The framework also delivers a 6.59x gain in token efficiency and a 5x reduction in training time, making it well-suited to resource-limited settings. During extreme training, DR. SAF can even surpass traditional instruction-based models in token efficiency with more than 16% accuracy improvement.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces the Dynamic Reasoning-Boundary Self-Awareness Framework (DR. SAF) to improve the efficiency of large language models (LLMs) in complex reasoning tasks.  DR. SAF dynamically adjusts reasoning depth based on problem complexity, enabling models to optimize their reasoning processes. The framework integrates three components: Boundary Self-Awareness Alignment, Adaptive Reward Management, and a Boundary Preservation Mechanism.  The experiments demonstrate significant reductions in token usage and training time while maintaining or improving accuracy.  The key idea is that models should be aware of their capabilities and adjust their reasoning "effort" accordingly, rather than relying on fixed human-defined priors about problem difficulty.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of dynamic adjustment of reasoning depth based on *model self-awareness* is relatively novel, especially in contrast to static methods, which are often human-defined difficulty priors. While other works have explored dynamic reasoning or early exits, the emphasis on aligning with the model's *intrinsic* capabilities is a key differentiator. Existing methods tend to be human-designed or heuristic-based, lacking the model's own self-assessed difficulty. The three components of DR. SAF (BSA, ALM, and BPM) seem reasonably designed to address the problem, though the specific implementations may benefit from further investigation into their individual contributions. Also, the use of GRPO isn't novel here, only used as an optimizer.
*   **Significance:** The potential for substantial efficiency gains (token reduction, faster training) is significant, as it directly addresses a key limitation of large LLMs – their computational cost. The paper convincingly shows that DR. SAF achieves state-of-the-art token efficiency on several mathematical reasoning benchmarks. Also, that DR.SAF surpass the performance of instruction-based models during the extreme training is exciting. The benefits are especially important in resource-constrained environments. This type of dynamic resource allocation based on self-awareness could become increasingly crucial as models grow even larger. It's a step towards more "intelligent" resource utilization.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   Strong empirical results: The paper demonstrates impressive gains across multiple benchmarks.
    *   Clear problem formulation: The paper clearly identifies and articulates the problem of static difficulty priors leading to inefficiencies.
    *   Well-defined framework: DR. SAF provides a structured approach to address the problem.
    *   Good ablations: The analysis of the contribution of different components (while not perfect - see weaknesses) is helpful.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The ablation study, while present, could be improved.  While there are ablations, there's no study about how each ablation affect each component in DR. SAF.
    *   Mathematical Proof Limited: The paper does contain a mathematical proof for adaptive length management and the use of Truncated Advantage to address the problem of Boundary Collapse, but only provides theoretical analysis limited to those components. It would be useful to get the mathematical proof over the contribution of BSA.
    *   Limited Generalizability Testing. While the paper tests the framework using mathematical datasets, a more comprehensive test must include the use of common sense benchmark datasets. This is a vital contribution to address potential limitations of using the framework in different environments.
*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has a solid chance of influencing the research direction towards more adaptive and self-aware LLMs. It demonstrates a path to improve efficiency without sacrificing accuracy, a critical consideration for the continued scaling and deployment of these models.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper offers a relatively novel and potentially significant approach to improving LLM efficiency through dynamic self-awareness. The empirical results are compelling, and the framework is reasonably well-defined. However, the contribution is somewhat incremental (building upon existing work on dynamic reasoning) and the evaluation has some limitations (especially the need for generalizability testing and more robust ablation analysis of individual components). I'm scoring on the lower end to acknowledge potential limitations for the framework.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### CryptoScope: Utilizing Large Language Models for Automated Cryptographic Logic Vulnerability Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11599v1)
- **Authors**: Zhihao Li, Zimo Ji, Tao Zheng, Hao Ren, Xiao Lan
- **Abstract**: Cryptographic algorithms are fundamental to modern security, yet their implementations frequently harbor subtle logic flaws that are hard to detect. We introduce CryptoScope, a novel framework for automated cryptographic vulnerability detection powered by Large Language Models (LLMs). CryptoScope combines Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting with Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), guided by a curated cryptographic knowledge base containing over 12,000 entries. We evaluate CryptoScope on LLM-CLVA, a benchmark of 92 cases primarily derived from real-world CVE vulnerabilities, complemented by cryptographic challenges from major Capture The Flag (CTF) competitions and synthetic examples across 11 programming languages. CryptoScope consistently improves performance over strong LLM baselines, boosting DeepSeek-V3 by 11.62%, GPT-4o-mini by 20.28%, and GLM-4-Flash by 28.69%. Additionally, it identifies 9 previously undisclosed flaws in widely used open-source cryptographic projects.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CRYPTOSCOPE, a novel framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) to automatically detect cryptographic logic vulnerabilities. It combines Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting with Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) and a curated cryptographic knowledge base. The framework's performance is evaluated on LLM-CLVA, a new benchmark dataset created by the authors containing real-world CVEs, CTF challenges, and synthetic examples. The results show that CRYPTOSCOPE consistently improves the performance of strong LLM baselines. Furthermore, the tool was used to discover previously unknown flaws in open-source cryptographic projects.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a genuinely novel approach to cryptographic vulnerability detection. While prior work has explored LLMs for security tasks, CRYPTOSCOPE stands out by specifically targeting *cryptographic logic vulnerabilities* (as opposed to API misuse) and by combining CoT and RAG in a structured way. The LLM-CLVA benchmark is also a valuable contribution, filling a gap in resources for evaluating LLMs in this domain. The discovery of new vulnerabilities in real-world projects lends further credibility to the proposed approach. It's also important to acknowledge that prior studies have used LLMs in code analysis, but the application to *cryptographic logic* is more targeted. The architecture combines these pieces in a new, clever way.

*   **Significance:** Cryptographic vulnerabilities are a significant threat, and existing automated tools often struggle to detect subtle logic flaws. CRYPTOSCOPE addresses this challenge by leveraging the reasoning abilities of LLMs in a systematic way. The language-agnostic nature of the framework is another important advantage. The demonstrated ability to find previously unknown vulnerabilities highlights the potential impact of this work. The LLM-CLVA benchmark will be an important asset to future research. A limitation of the current significance, however, is that it is an LLM tool, meaning it might be limited by the cost and limitations of LLMs. Additionally, while the system detects vulnerabilities, the system likely still relies on expert judgement for confirming vulnerabilities.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined framework combining CoT, RAG, and a custom knowledge base.
    *   Comprehensive evaluation on a newly created benchmark dataset.
    *   Demonstrated ability to discover real-world vulnerabilities.
    *   Language agnostic approach
    *   Rigorous ablations.
    *   The creation of the benchmark is a major contribution.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could benefit from more detailed analysis of the types of vulnerabilities that CRYPTOSCOPE is best at detecting and the types that it struggles with.
    *   The evaluation primarily focuses on improving LLM performance on a benchmark, while more detailed case studies of the discovered vulnerabilities would strengthen the impact.
    *   The practical considerations and limitations of using LLMs (e.g., computational cost, API limitations, reliance on external services, need for expert judgement) are not discussed in detail.
    *   The impact of the quality of the curated knowledge base is not evaluated rigorously, potentially limiting insight on the long-term maintenance of the system.
    *   The method could be vulnerable to adversarial code or natural language instructions.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in automated security analysis, particularly in the application of LLMs to vulnerability detection. The LLM-CLVA benchmark is likely to become a standard resource for evaluating new approaches in this area. The open-source release of CRYPTOSCOPE could also facilitate adoption and further development by the security community.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** CRYPTOSCOPE represents a valuable contribution to the field of automated cryptographic vulnerability detection. The novelty lies in the specific combination of LLM techniques (CoT and RAG) tailored for logic flaws, the language agnosticism of the approach, and in the new benchmark dataset. The discovery of real-world vulnerabilities strengthens the significance. The work is well-executed and evaluated. However, the relatively limited analysis of vulnerability types, practical considerations and potential limitations prevent a higher score. The reliance on LLMs, and the associated risks, is another consideration. Despite these limitations, the work is solid and sets a good foundation for future research.

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 7/10

### TinyTim: A Family of Language Models for Divergent Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11607v1)
- **Authors**: Christopher J. Agostino
- **Abstract**: This work introduces TinyTim, a family of large language models fine-tuned on James Joyce's `Finnegans Wake'. Through quantitative evaluation against baseline models, we demonstrate that TinyTim V1 produces a statistically distinct generative profile characterized by high lexical diversity and low semantic coherence. These findings are interpreted through theories of creativity and complex problem-solving, arguing that such specialized models can function as divergent knowledge sources within more extensive creative architectures, powering automated discovery mechanisms in diverse settings.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the TinyTim paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces TinyTim, a family of large language models fine-tuned exclusively on James Joyce's *Finnegans Wake*. The authors argue that this specialized training produces a model with a divergent generative profile, characterized by high lexical diversity and low semantic coherence. This contrasts with standard LLMs, which tend toward convergent, "mean-reverting" outputs.  The paper presents quantitative evaluations demonstrating TinyTim's statistically distinct generative characteristics compared to baseline LLMs trained for coherence. The authors interpret these findings through the lens of creativity theories, suggesting that TinyTim-like models could serve as divergent knowledge sources within larger creative architectures, facilitating automated discovery.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*Novelty:* The primary novelty lies in the deliberate engineering of a *divergent* language model through fine-tuning on highly unconventional, associative text. While fine-tuning LLMs is a common practice, the specific choice of *Finnegans Wake* and the explicit goal of fostering divergence are relatively unique. Comparing TinyTim to standard coherent models is a clear and accessible way to evaluate it.

*Significance:* The significance of this work hinges on its potential to shift the focus of LLM research beyond mere coherence and accuracy toward exploring their role in creative processes.  The paper's emphasis on divergent thinking and human-AI co-creation raises interesting questions about how LLMs can be integrated into creative workflows. However, the demonstration remains somewhat preliminary. While quantitative metrics are provided, the *practical* utility of TinyTim in real-world creative tasks is not fully explored.  The discussion on challenging parsimony is intriguing, highlighting the potential benefits of complex models for navigating complex problem spaces. The link between divergent knowledge models and human-AI partnerships is promising.

*Strengths:*
*   Clear hypothesis and experimental design.
*   Quantitative evaluation with appropriate metrics (Unique Word Ratio, Yule's K, etc.)
*   Compelling interpretation of results within the framework of creativity theories.
*   Focus on an area of AI creativity that is less investigated and could be very impactful.

*Weaknesses:*
*   Limited exploration of practical applications. The paper hints at the potential for creative collaboration, but doesn't provide concrete examples.
*   The "divergence" is largely quantified through lexical novelty. Semantic coherence is low, but the paper could delve more deeply into the *type* of incoherence and whether it is creatively useful (e.g., generating novel metaphors or unexpected juxtapositions).
*   The baseline models, while standard, could be more extensive. Comparisons with other models trained on more "experimental" literature might be useful.
*   The claim that LLMs are inherently parsimonious needs more nuanced argument - model size and complexity is rapidly increasing.

*Potential Influence:* The paper could influence researchers to explore:
*   Alternative training datasets for promoting divergent thinking in LLMs.
*   Architectures that combine convergent and divergent models for creative problem-solving.
*   Metrics beyond standard accuracy and coherence for evaluating creative AI.
*   Human-AI collaboration methods that leverage LLMs as sources of unexpected insights.

*Rigorous Rationale for Score*: While the paper presents a novel approach and raises important questions, its impact is limited by the preliminary nature of the demonstration and the lack of concrete applications. The concept of a divergent language model is innovative, but more work is needed to translate this divergence into tangible benefits for creative tasks. Therefore, I am awarding a score of 6. The work is well-presented and has a clear impact, but the exploration of its possibilities is limited.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### Controlling Multimodal LLMs via Reward-guided Decoding
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11616v1)
- **Authors**: Oscar Mañas, Pierluca D'Oro, Koustuv Sinha, Adriana Romero-Soriano, Michal Drozdzal, Aishwarya Agrawal
- **Abstract**: As Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) gain widespread applicability, it is becoming increasingly desirable to adapt them for diverse user needs. In this paper, we study the adaptation of MLLMs through controlled decoding. To achieve this, we introduce the first method for reward-guided decoding of MLLMs and demonstrate its application in improving their visual grounding. Our method involves building reward models for visual grounding and using them to guide the MLLM's decoding process. Concretely, we build two separate reward models to independently control the degree of object precision and recall in the model's output. Our approach enables on-the-fly controllability of an MLLM's inference process in two ways: first, by giving control over the relative importance of each reward function during decoding, allowing a user to dynamically trade off object precision for recall in image captioning tasks; second, by giving control over the breadth of the search during decoding, allowing the user to control the trade-off between the amount of test-time compute and the degree of visual grounding. We evaluate our method on standard object hallucination benchmarks, showing that it provides significant controllability over MLLM inference, while consistently outperforming existing hallucination mitigation methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel method called Multimodal Reward-Guided Decoding (MRGD) for improving the controllability of Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) during inference. MRGD uses two reward models, one focused on reducing object hallucination (increasing precision) and another on improving object recall. By combining these reward models with a tunable weight parameter (w), the method enables users to control the trade-off between precision and recall in the MLLM's outputs. Additionally, the method allows for controlling the balance between test-time compute and the quality of visual grounding. The authors evaluate MRGD on standard object hallucination benchmarks, demonstrating its ability to outperform existing hallucination mitigation techniques while providing greater control over MLLM behavior at inference time.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application of reward-guided decoding to MLLMs, particularly with specifically designed reward models for addressing the unique challenges of multimodal hallucination. While reward-guided decoding is known for text-only LLMs, the extension to the multimodal setting and the creation of separate reward models for precision and recall constitute a genuine contribution. The ability to trade-off object precision and recall in MLLM outputs is a significant advancement as well.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in MLLMs: the lack of fine-grained control over their outputs. The ability to adapt MLLM behavior to diverse user needs (e.g., high precision for visually impaired users, high recall for data generation) has important practical implications. The demonstrated improvement over existing hallucination mitigation techniques further strengthens the significance of the work.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the need for controllable MLLMs and identifies specific user requirements.
    *   **Methodological Soundness:** MRGD is a well-defined and technically sound approach. The use of separate reward models for precision and recall is a clever design choice.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The authors conduct thorough experiments on standard benchmarks, comparing MRGD against a range of baselines and ablation studies to demonstrate its effectiveness.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The method's controllability and ability to reduce hallucinations make it highly relevant for real-world applications of MLLMs.
    *   **Well-Written:** The paper is clear, well-organized, and easy to follow.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Modules:** The object recall reward model (rrec) relies on off-the-shelf modules, which may limit its performance. The paper doesn't explore the impact of these modules in depth.
    *   **Limited Scope of Hallucinations Addressed:** The paper focuses primarily on object hallucinations.  While this is a significant area,  other types of multimodal hallucinations (e.g., attribute, spatial relationships) are not considered.  Future work acknowledges this, but it constrains the paper's overall impact.
    *   **Computational Cost:** While the paper claims improved sample efficiency against rejection sampling, the method still involves sampling and evaluating multiple candidate responses, leading to a higher computational cost than greedy decoding.
    *   **Suboptimal Reward Model Performance**: While the reward model achieves 82.05% accuracy, the paper indicates it is "in line with well-behaved reward models", which suggests there's room to improve the reward models to drive even better MLLM outputs.
    *   **Lack of novelty of combining different reward models (hallucination and recall)** Combining different reward models is not a completely new concept (e.g., [12] combines it with classifier-free guidance).
    *   **Limited theoretical grounding**: The study primarily focuses on empirical findings and lacks in-depth theoretical analysis of the reward function and hallucination trade-offs.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper could significantly impact how MLLMs are deployed in various applications. The controllability offered by MRGD could make MLLMs more reliable and adaptable to diverse user needs, promoting their wider adoption. The approach may also inspire further research into reward-guided decoding and other techniques for controlling MLLMs.

*   **Overall Assessment:** The paper provides a valuable contribution by adapting reward-guided decoding to address hallucination issues in MLLMs. The introduction of separate reward models for object precision and recall, coupled with the ability to control their relative influence during inference, is a novel and practical approach. While the paper has some limitations (e.g., reliance on pre-trained modules, limited scope of hallucinations), its strengths outweigh its weaknesses, making it a significant contribution to the field.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** A score of 7.5 reflects the paper's significant contribution to the field of multimodal learning. It's innovative in its application of reward-guided decoding to MLLMs, particularly with tailored reward models. The impact of MRGD on controllability and hallucination reduction is clearly demonstrated in experiments.  While there are some limitations regarding the reward model accuracy, the reliance on pre-trained module, only limited addressing of hallucinations, and lack of theoretical grounding, the paper's strengths, especially the practical relevance and comprehensive evaluation, warrant a high score. The work opens up promising avenues for future research and development in controllable MLLMs.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LoRAtorio: An intrinsic approach to LoRA Skill Composition
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11624v1)
- **Authors**: Niki Foteinopoulou, Ignas Budvytis, Stephan Liwicki
- **Abstract**: Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) has become a widely adopted technique in text-to-image diffusion models, enabling the personalisation of visual concepts such as characters, styles, and objects. However, existing approaches struggle to effectively compose multiple LoRA adapters, particularly in open-ended settings where the number and nature of required skills are not known in advance. In this work, we present LoRAtorio, a novel train-free framework for multi-LoRA composition that leverages intrinsic model behaviour. Our method is motivated by two key observations: (1) LoRA adapters trained on narrow domains produce denoised outputs that diverge from the base model, and (2) when operating out-of-distribution, LoRA outputs show behaviour closer to the base model than when conditioned in distribution. The balance between these two observations allows for exceptional performance in the single LoRA scenario, which nevertheless deteriorates when multiple LoRAs are loaded. Our method operates in the latent space by dividing it into spatial patches and computing cosine similarity between each patch's predicted noise and that of the base model. These similarities are used to construct a spatially-aware weight matrix, which guides a weighted aggregation of LoRA outputs. To address domain drift, we further propose a modification to classifier-free guidance that incorporates the base model's unconditional score into the composition. We extend this formulation to a dynamic module selection setting, enabling inference-time selection of relevant LoRA adapters from a large pool. LoRAtorio achieves state-of-the-art performance, showing up to a 1.3% improvement in ClipScore and a 72.43% win rate in GPT-4V pairwise evaluations, and generalises effectively to multiple latent diffusion models.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "LoRAtorio: An intrinsic approach to LoRA Skill Composition":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LoRAtorio, a novel, train-free framework for composing multiple LoRA adapters in text-to-image diffusion models. It addresses the challenge of performance degradation when combining multiple LoRAs, particularly in open-ended settings. LoRAtorio leverages the intrinsic behavior of LoRA-augmented models, observing that LoRA outputs diverge from the base model's outputs when trained on narrow domains, but converge towards the base model's behavior when operating out-of-distribution.  The method operates in the latent space, dividing it into spatial patches and computing cosine similarity between each patch's denoised representation and that of the base model. These similarities are used to construct a spatially-aware weight matrix for weighted aggregation of LoRA outputs. To further mitigate domain drift, a modification to classifier-free guidance is proposed, incorporating the base model's unconditional score.  The framework extends to a dynamic module selection setting, enabling inference-time selection of relevant LoRA adapters. Experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on the ComposLoRA benchmark and generalization to multiple latent diffusion models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a genuinely novel approach to LoRA composition. The core idea of leveraging the intrinsic behavior of LoRAs (divergence and convergence relative to the base model based on input domain) is insightful. The spatially-aware weighting and the re-centering of classifier-free guidance based on base model output are also novel and clever techniques. The extension to dynamic module selection enhances the method's practical applicability. Prior works have explored trainable mixture of experts, hypernetworks for LoRA weights, and schedule based approaches. The strength of this work is it avoids training, by leveraging intrinsic behavior of the LoRA and base model.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in the rapidly evolving field of personalized text-to-image generation. The ability to effectively combine multiple concepts represented by LoRAs is essential for creating complex and nuanced images. The performance gains demonstrated on the ComposLoRA benchmark are significant. The method's train-free nature is a substantial advantage in real-world applications where retraining or fine-tuning is often impractical. The generalization to different diffusion models (Stable Diffusion and Flux) strengthens the claim of broad applicability. The method uses intrinsic model behavior which is novel as it does not rely on prior task knowledge.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The core motivation and observations about LoRA behavior are compelling.
    *   The method is train-free, making it highly practical.
    *   The architecture is well-designed and technically sound.
    *   The experimental results are thorough and demonstrate significant performance improvements.
    *   The generalization to multiple diffusion models is a strong validation.
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the approach and its rationale.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The computational cost, increasing linearly with the number of LoRAs, is a limitation, particularly in dynamic settings. The authors acknowledge this.
    *   The method relies on reasonably well-aligned and semantically coherent LoRA datasets. Performance could degrade if the input LoRAs are of low quality or poorly trained. There seems to be some reliance on the base model's ability, as stated "Finally, we note that the quality of images is affected by the base model".
    *   The reliance of all LoRAs from ComposLoRA or Flux, all without access to training details emphasizes that "all results should be interpreted in light of this uncertainty.".

*   **Potential Influence:** The LoRAtorio framework has the potential to significantly impact the field of text-to-image generation. Its train-free nature and ability to effectively compose multiple concepts make it a valuable tool for content creators and researchers alike. The method could inspire further research into leveraging intrinsic model behaviors for various generative tasks.
*   **Justification:** While the linear increase in computational cost with each LoRA may seem like a huge drawback, it is in line with performance gain. Moreover, this problem has already been addressed and noted as an active item to address. The method has state of the art performance on all existing benchmarks. Finally, its generalizability to different diffusion models further makes this a strong paper.

**Score: 8**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to LoRA composition, with clear practical advantages. The approach is well-motivated, technically sound, and experimentally validated. While the computational cost is a limitation, the overall strengths of the paper, particularly its novel approach and significant performance improvements, justify a score of 8. The limitations are already addressed in the paper and should be areas of focus in next iterations.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Is ChatGPT-5 Ready for Mammogram VQA?
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.11628v1)
- **Authors**: Qiang Li, Shansong Wang, Mingzhe Hu, Mojtaba Safari, Zachary Eidex, Xiaofeng Yang
- **Abstract**: Mammogram visual question answering (VQA) integrates image interpretation with clinical reasoning and has potential to support breast cancer screening. We systematically evaluated the GPT-5 family and GPT-4o model on four public mammography datasets (EMBED, InBreast, CMMD, CBIS-DDSM) for BI-RADS assessment, abnormality detection, and malignancy classification tasks. GPT-5 consistently was the best performing model but lagged behind both human experts and domain-specific fine-tuned models. On EMBED, GPT-5 achieved the highest scores among GPT variants in density (56.8%), distortion (52.5%), mass (64.5%), calcification (63.5%), and malignancy (52.8%) classification. On InBreast, it attained 36.9% BI-RADS accuracy, 45.9% abnormality detection, and 35.0% malignancy classification. On CMMD, GPT-5 reached 32.3% abnormality detection and 55.0% malignancy accuracy. On CBIS-DDSM, it achieved 69.3% BI-RADS accuracy, 66.0% abnormality detection, and 58.2% malignancy accuracy. Compared with human expert estimations, GPT-5 exhibited lower sensitivity (63.5%) and specificity (52.3%). While GPT-5 exhibits promising capabilities for screening tasks, its performance remains insufficient for high-stakes clinical imaging applications without targeted domain adaptation and optimization. However, the tremendous improvements in performance from GPT-4o to GPT-5 show a promising trend in the potential for general large language models (LLMs) to assist with mammography VQA tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the zero-shot performance of the GPT-5 family of models, particularly GPT-5, on mammogram visual question answering (VQA) tasks.  The authors evaluated these models on four publicly available mammography datasets (EMBED, InBreast, CMMD, CBIS-DDSM) for BI-RADS assessment, abnormality detection, and malignancy classification. The study compares the performance of GPT-5, its smaller variants (mini, nano), and the previous generation GPT-40 against each other, human expert estimations, and some domain-specific, fine-tuned models. The results show that GPT-5 consistently outperformed its smaller variants but still lagged behind both human experts and specialized AI systems. The authors conclude that while GPT-5 shows promise, significant improvements are needed in domain adaptation and reasoning transparency for high-stakes clinical imaging applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its timely evaluation of the GPT-5 family on a specific and clinically relevant task – mammogram VQA. While previous works have explored general VQA or VQA in other medical imaging modalities, the application to mammography using the very latest generation of LLMs (specifically attempting to assess GPT-5) provides a novel contribution. However, the *methodology* isn't particularly innovative. They use a zero-shot approach with a fairly standard CoT prompting. The datasets are existing and publicly available. The VQA question generation is automated from existing structured labels, which, while practical, limits the depth of image understanding tested. So, it is testing application rather than new method.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the potential impact of AI on breast cancer screening. Even if GPT-5 falls short of expert performance, understanding its capabilities and limitations is valuable. The study highlights the gap between general-purpose LLMs and domain-specific expertise, underscoring the necessity of fine-tuning and/or specialized architectures for medical imaging applications. The quantification of performance differences across different model sizes within the GPT-5 family is also useful for future research directions.

**Strengths:**
*   **Timeliness:** Evaluating the very latest model, GPT-5, is important to understand if advancements in LLMs translate to improvements on specialized tasks.
*   **Systematic evaluation:** The study uses multiple datasets and performance metrics, providing a relatively comprehensive assessment of the models' abilities.
*   **Comparison to human experts:** Benchmarking the AI against human performance is crucial for determining its clinical utility.
*   **Clear limitations:** The authors are transparent about the limitations of their study, including the zero-shot setting, the use of pre-defined question templates, and the absence of detailed error analysis.
*   **Identifies key challenges:** The discussion accurately highlights the difficulties associated with mammogram interpretation, such as the subtle nature of features and the need to integrate patient history and prior examinations.

**Weaknesses:**
*   **Limited methodology:** The zero-shot approach and use of auto-generated questions limit the depth of the investigation. Fine-tuning and more complex question generation strategies could yield more insightful results.
*   **Lack of in-depth error analysis:** A more granular analysis of failure modes (e.g., types of lesions misclassified, reasons for incorrect density assessments) would provide valuable insights for improving model performance. The limited case studies only scratch the surface.
*   **Missing comparison to specific fine-tuned VQA models for mammography:** The comparison to generic fine-tuned SOTA models is useful, but a comparison to state-of-the-art *mammogram VQA* models would strengthen the evaluation.
*   **Dependency on OpenAI availability:** The paper's conclusions and applicability depend on continued access to the GPT-5 model and its potential future changes.

**Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the field by:
*   Motivating further research on fine-tuning LLMs for mammography VQA.
*   Encouraging the development of more sophisticated VQA question generation strategies for medical imaging.
*   Highlighting the need for domain-specific architectures or training methods to improve performance on complex imaging tasks.
*   Serving as a benchmark for future evaluations of LLMs in medical imaging.

**Score:** 6

**Rationale:** While the paper tackles a timely and relevant topic and offers a valuable initial assessment of GPT-5 on mammogram VQA, its methodological limitations significantly limit its overall impact. The zero-shot approach and the use of predefined question templates provide a somewhat superficial evaluation. A more comprehensive exploration involving fine-tuning, more complex question types, and in-depth error analysis would have significantly increased the paper's novelty and significance. The conclusions are somewhat predictable (GPT-5 is better than previous versions but still not as good as fine-tuned systems and human experts).  The key contribution is the empirical evidence supporting this claim, but the lack of methodological depth and a limited comparison to established mammography VQA research keeps the score moderate.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10
